   604	
   605	
   606	
   607	
   608	#: 形如"harness 自己调模型时连不上"的失败痕迹。
   609	#:
   610	#: 为什么要单独认这一类:实测(2026-10-02,`loop-terminal_bench-submit-1`)两道题的
   611	#: harness 死在 `RuntimeError: 3 attempts failed, last: Connection error.`,而平台给它们
   612	#: 记的是 **0.0** —— 也就是把一次**网关故障**记成了"harness 很弱"。§2.5.7 的全部主张
   613	#: 就是"测不了的题"和"测出来是 0 的题"必须分开,这一条正是它要防的那种混淆。
   614	#:
   615	#: 机制已经复现过(见 `eval/service/modelgate.py` 的说明):vLLM 的 uvicorn 默认 5 秒关掉空闲
   616	#: keep-alive,而网关把上游的 EOF 半关到客户端,于是 harness 隔十几秒的下一次调用
   617	#: 复用了一条死连接 —— 只要中间跑过一条几十秒的命令就会踩到。
   618	_PROVIDER_FAILURES = (
   619	    "APIConnectionError", "APITimeoutError", "Connection error",
   620	    "RemoteDisconnected", "Connection refused", "Connection reset",
   621	    "connect timeout", "Server disconnected",
   622	)
   623	
   624	
   625	def _provider_failure(result: dict) -> dict | None:
   626	    """harness 是不是**因为连不上模型端点**而死的。`None` 表示不是。
   627	
   628	    判据刻意很窄,只在这种情况成立:
   629	
   630	      * harness 以**非零退出码**结束 —— 它自己没把这次故障吸收掉;
   631	      * stderr 的**尾部**有客户端连接/超时的痕迹 —— 尾部,不是全文:一个连上又掉了、
   632	        但自己重试成功的 harness 会在中间留下同样的字眼,而那是它做对了。
   633	
   634	    退出码 0 的一律不算:它跑完了,分数就是它该得的分数。
   635	    """
   636	    if not result.get("exit_code"):            # None(无效)或 0 都不算
   637	        return None
   638	    tail = (result.get("stderr") or "")[-1500:]
   639	    for marker in _PROVIDER_FAILURES:
   640	        if marker in tail:
   641	            # 摘出那一行,放进记录里 —— 读者要的是"哪一句"。
   642	            line = next((l.strip() for l in reversed(tail.splitlines())
   643	                         if marker in l), marker)
   644	            return {"marker": marker, "line": line[:240],
   645	                    "exit_code": result.get("exit_code")}
   646	    return None
   647	
   648	
   649	def _invalid(task: dict, exc, *, stage: str, service: str | None) -> dict:
   650	    """A task the platform could not measure, as opposed to one it measured as zero.
   651	
   652	    INTERFACE.md §2.5.7: the result is **not** a `per_task` score and **not** a zero,
   653	    and it is attributed to the dataset and the service by name. The distinction is the
   654	    entire reason this platform exists — a broken mock API and a weak harness must not
   655	    be the same number — and it is independently attested: JarvisBench reports that
   656	    "harness or provider failures are marked invalid rather than assigned a score of
   657	    zero" (`INTERFACE.md` §2.5.7).
   658	    """
   659	    return {"task_id": task["task_id"], "invalid": {
   660	                "stage": stage, "service": service, "detail": str(exc)},
   661	            "answer": "", "trace": "", "exit_code": None, "stderr": "",
   662	            "env_usage": {}, "model_gateway": None, "identity": None}
   663	
   664	
   665	
   666	
   667	def _spread(xs: list[float]) -> float:
   668	    """Population standard deviation, with no dependency and no ambiguity.
   669	
   670	    The population form because these are *all* the trials this run took, not a sample
   671	    from a larger population of runs -- `statistics.pstdev` is the honest name for it, and
   672	    the difference from `stdev` matters most at small n, which is exactly where a reader
   673	    compares two harnesses.
   674	    """
   675	    if len(xs) < 2:
   676	        return 0.0
   677	    mu = sum(xs) / len(xs)
   678	    return (sum((x - mu) ** 2 for x in xs) / len(xs)) ** 0.5
   679	
   680	
   681	def _harness_failure(result: dict) -> dict | None:
   682	    """Why a harness left nothing to read, when it left nothing.
   683	
   684	    A task can be **measured** and still say nothing about the harness: the check reads
   685	    the artifact, finds it wrong, and records a 0.0 -- and that 0.0 is a real score. But
   686	    if the harness *also* wrote no trace, then no method has anything to read while the
   687	    curve reports a number, and those two situations are indistinguishable on the curve.
   688	
   689	    Measured, and the reason this exists: a harness that needed a package the task image
   690	    did not have died on its first model call with `ModuleNotFoundError`, exit code 1.
   691	    The trace was 0 bytes, the score was 0.000, and the run reported *nothing* -- the
   692	    exit code and stderr were computed here and then dropped, so "the method decided not
   693	    to edit" was the only reading available. A harness that cannot start is a fact about
   694	    the run and belongs in the record.
   695	
   696	    Every harness the platform ships writes `trace.jsonl`, so an empty one is always
   697	    abnormal; a harness that simply chose to write nothing is worth knowing about too.
   698	    Deliberately **not** folded into `invalid`: the task was measured, so it keeps its
   699	    score. This is the attribution, not a reason to drop the measurement (§2.5.7).
   700	
   701	    **The second trigger, and the measured case that forced it.** A non-empty trace is
   702	    not proof that the harness ran. Measured 2026-10-04 on the current platform:
   703	    `--harness cli_agent` with no CLI writes exactly one line,
   704	    `{"error": "HG_CLI is unset: no CLI to run"}`, never calls a model, exits 2 -- and the
   705	    curve said `score 0.0, harness_failed: null, invalid: null`. The console warned and
   706	    the verdict file carried the exit code, so the fact existed in two places and not on
   707	    the point, which is where a reader compares runs.
   708	
   709	    So: a harness that **exited non-zero without recording a single model call** is a
   710	    harness failure too. "A model call" is read from the `usage` block, which the harness
   711	    contract requires (`INTERFACE.md` §1.2) -- the platform is entitled to rely on it, and
   712	    it is the only schema-independent evidence that a harness talked to a model. A
   713	    scripted harness that legitimately makes no calls (`base_harness/oracle`) exits 0 and
   714	    is not flagged.
   715	    """
   716	    trace = (result.get("trace") or "").strip()
   717	    reason = None
   718	    if not trace:
   719	        reason = "the harness left no trace at all"
   720	    elif result.get("exit_code") and _last_usage(trace) is None:
   721	        # **只说平台知道的事。** 第一版这条写的是"没有记录到任何模型调用",而那是错的:
   722	        # `usage` 块是 harness 跑完之后才写一次的,所以**中途崩溃**永远看起来像"一次都没调
   723	        # 过模型"。实测(2026-10-04,本地模型,5 道题):三道题各有 15-23 次调用、轨迹 12-14 万
   724	        # 字节,控制台却按"left no trace"播报 —— 一句假话,而且是我自己上一版引入的。
   725	        reason = (f"the harness exited {result['exit_code']} and its trace has no `usage` "
   726	                  f"block, so the run did not reach its end")
   727	    if reason is None:
   728	        return None
   729	    return {"reason": reason, "exit_code": result.get("exit_code"),
   730	            "stderr": (result.get("stderr") or "")[-1500:]}
   731	
   732	
   733	def _reporter():
   734	    """The reporter an evaluation should narrate through.
   735	
   736	    The context first, then the console's own. Both are needed and neither is
   737	    redundant:
   738	
   739	    * the **context** is how a task's phases reach the code that runs them
   740	      (`eval/containers/container.py` beats while it waits inside a single `docker exec`);
   741	    * the **console** is what makes that context exist at all when the driver has not
   742	      set one. Measured: the driver's own calls to `evaluate` (`driver.py` round 0, the
   743	      mode B loop, the eval side) run on the main thread with no context set, and the
   744	      first version of this put the progress events *only* in the context -- so a real
   745	      run reported the method call's phases and nothing at all for the four tasks it
   746	      spent the hour on. A run that narrates its shortest wait and hides its longest
   747	      is worse than one that narrates neither.
   748	    """
   749	    from eval.observe.console import CONSOLE
   750	    return progress_mod.current() or CONSOLE.reporter
   751	
   752	
   753	def evaluate(repo: Path, tasks: list[dict], scorable: dict[str, str],
   754	             cache: dict | None = None, harness_sha: str = "",
   755	             sandbox: bool = True, setups: dict | None = None,
   756	             verifiers: dict | None = None,
   757	             envs: dict | None = None,
   758	             run_id: str = "", run_seed: str = "",
   759	             recordings_root: Path | None = None,
   760	             round_no: int = 0, trials: int = 1, jobs: int = 1,
   761	             progress_dir: Path | None = None,
   762	             diagnostic_authorization: Authorization | None = None) -> dict:
   763	    """Run a harness over a task set and score it.
   764	
   765	    `scorable` maps task_id -> expected answer. Scoring lives here, not in the
   766	    Trainer, because a subject that scores itself destroys the comparison.
   767	
   768	    Two functions so that the whole evaluation -- not just each task's own phases --
   769	    runs inside one reporting context, which is what lets the container client beat
   770	    while it waits on a `docker exec`. The signature and the body are the same as
   771	    before; only the `with` is new.
   772	    """
   773	    with progress_mod.reporting(_reporter()):
   774	        result = _evaluate(repo, tasks, scorable, cache=cache, harness_sha=harness_sha,
   775	                         sandbox=sandbox, setups=setups, verifiers=verifiers,
   776	                         envs=envs, run_id=run_id, run_seed=run_seed,
   777	                         recordings_root=recordings_root, round_no=round_no,
   778	                         trials=trials, jobs=jobs, progress_dir=progress_dir,
   779	                         diagnostic_authorization=diagnostic_authorization)
   780	        if diagnostic_authorization is not None:
   781	            result["enforcement"] = diagnostic_authorization.record()
   782	        return result
   783	
   784	
   785	def _in_parallel(tasks: list[dict], measure, jobs: int) -> dict[str, dict]:
   786	    """Measure every task, up to `jobs` at a time, and collect the outcomes by task id.
   787	
   788	    `jobs=1` stays a plain sequential loop, because the platform's default record must not
   789	    depend on a thread pool to be the record it has always been.
   790	
   791	    Why this axis and not another: on a measured three-task run the agent phase was 87.8% of
   792	    the wall clock (2578 s of 2936 s) and the platform sat idle for all of it -- the tasks are
   793	    independent by construction (own container, own network, own model gateway, own workspace,
   794	    own cache key), so this is the cheap parallelism. Splitting *inside* a task is not
   795	    available: the harness is a process the platform does not control.
   796	
   797	    The first exception is re-raised after cancelling what has not started. A task already
   798	    running is left to finish: `run_one` tears its own containers down in a `finally`, and
   799	    abandoning a thread mid-teardown is how a leaked network gets inherited by whatever runs
   800	    next (§2.5.7).
   801	    """
   802	    if jobs <= 1:
   803	        # Computed in task order, which is what makes the merge reproducible at one
   804	        # `--jobs` setting as well as comparable across two of them.
   805	        return {task["task_id"]: measure(index, task)
   806	                for index, task in enumerate(tasks, start=1)}
   807	    pool = futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=jobs, thread_name_prefix="hg-task")
   808	    pending = [pool.submit(measure, index, task)
   809	               for index, task in enumerate(tasks, start=1)]
   810	    outcomes: dict[str, dict] = {}
   811	    try:
   812	        for done in futures.as_completed(pending):
   813	            outcome = done.result()
   814	            outcomes[outcome["task_id"]] = outcome
   815	            # **Stop paying for a harness that cannot start.** See `_all_unstarted`: the
   816	            # failure is task-independent, so `jobs` agreements are enough, and the rest
   817	            # of the round can only return the same `0.000` at full price.
   818	            if _all_unstarted(outcomes, jobs):
   819	                for fut in pending:
   820	                    fut.cancel()
   821	                break
   822	    except BaseException:
   823	        pool.shutdown(wait=True, cancel_futures=True)
   824	        raise
   825	    pool.shutdown(wait=True)
   826	    # **A cancelled task is not a task that scored nothing.** The merge indexes `outcomes`
   827	    # by every task, so the cancelled ones are marked `not_run` and kept out of `per_task`
   828	    # rather than given a zero the platform never measured (§2.5.7).
   829	    for task in tasks:
   830	        outcomes.setdefault(task["task_id"],
   831	                            {"task_id": task["task_id"], "not_run": True})
   832	    return outcomes
   833	
   834	
   835	def _all_unstarted(outcomes: dict[str, dict], jobs: int) -> bool:
   836	    """Has the harness failed to *start* on every task measured so far, `jobs` of them?
   837	
   838	    This is the platform's own question (`_harness_failure`) asked about a round instead of
   839	    a task. A harness whose entrypoint cannot be invoked fails identically on every task --
   840	    that is the task-independent part of a run -- so `jobs` agreements are enough to stop
   841	    the round. Measured (`codex-dgm`, 2026-10-05): the improver's edit dropped the
   842	    `--task`/`--workdir` contract, all ten tasks produced `FileNotFoundError: '--task'`
   843	    with no trace, and that happened in **two consecutive rounds** -- twenty
   844	    task-measurements whose only possible contribution was `0.000` over and over.
   845	
   846	    Deliberately not "any task failed to start": a task-specific failure (an image with no
   847	    interpreter, say) is a fact about *that task*, and stopping the round on it would trade
   848	    one wrong record for another.
   849	    """
   850	    if len(outcomes) < jobs:
   851	        return False
   852	    for outcome in outcomes.values():
   853	        if outcome.get("not_run") or outcome.get("invalid") or "cached" in outcome:
   854	            return False
   855	        measured = outcome.get("measured") or []
   856	        if not measured or not _harness_failure(measured[-1]):
   857	            return False
   858	    return True
   859	
   860	
   861	def _evaluate(repo: Path, tasks: list[dict], scorable: dict[str, str],
   862	              cache: dict | None = None, harness_sha: str = "",
   863	              sandbox: bool = True, setups: dict | None = None,
   864	              verifiers: dict | None = None,
   865	              envs: dict | None = None,
   866	              round_no: int = 0,
   867	              run_id: str = "", run_seed: str = "",
   868	              progress_dir: Path | None = None,
   869	              recordings_root: Path | None = None,
   870	              trials: int = 1, jobs: int = 1,
   871	              diagnostic_authorization: Authorization | None = None) -> dict:
   872	    """Score a harness on a task set.
   873	
   874	    `trials` is how many times each `(harness, task)` pair is measured. One is the
   875	    default and one is not enough: measured on this platform, **the same unmodified
   876	    harness scored 0.333 in one run and 0.000 in the next**, because the agent model
   877	    intermittently emits a tool call in a dialect the harness cannot parse and the harness
   878	    then ends the task. Nothing about that is a property of the dataset; it is the floor of
   879	    what a single sample can tell you, and a curve that does not say how noisy it is cannot
   880	    support a claim that an edit helped. With `trials > 1` a task's score is the mean over
   881	    trials and `per_task_std` is their spread (population standard deviation).
   882	
   883	    `jobs` is how many tasks are measured **at the same time**, and it multiplies with
   884	    `trials` rather than replacing it: trials buy a spread, jobs buy wall clock. Both are
   885	    recorded -- `trials` on the point, `jobs` in the run's own parameters -- because a run
   886	    that used four containers at once is a different claim about this host than one that
   887	    used one.
   888	    """
   889	    if diagnostic_authorization is not None:
   890	        diagnostic_authorization.require_scope(
   891	            run_id, [t["task_id"] for t in tasks], trials=trials, jobs=jobs, round_no=round_no)
   892	        if cache:
   893	            raise ValueError("unobserved diagnostic requires an empty cache; no cached measurements")
   894	        # A later ordinary caller must not reuse a diagnostic's provisional score.
   895	        cache = {}
   896	    if trials < 1:
   897	        raise ValueError(f"trials must be at least 1, not {trials}")
   898	    if jobs < 1:
   899	        raise ValueError(f"jobs must be at least 1, not {jobs}")
   900	    evaluation_protocols = {task["task_id"]: protocol for task in tasks
   901	                            if (protocol := chess_stepwise.protocol_of(task)) is not None}
   902	    if len(set(evaluation_protocols.values())) > 1:
   903	        raise ValueError("cannot aggregate interactive and offline ChessBench protocols")
   904	
   905	    def measurement_key(task):
   906	        key = f"{harness_sha}:{task['task_id']}:{trials}"
   907	        # A changed scorer declaration must not reuse another runtime's score.
   908	        bundle = ((verifiers or {}).get(task["task_id"]) or {}).get("scorer_bundle")
   909	        if bundle is not None:
   910	            key += ":scorer:" + hashlib.sha256(json.dumps(
   911	                bundle, sort_keys=True, default=str).encode()).hexdigest()
   912	        if evaluation_protocols.get(task["task_id"]) == chess_stepwise.STEPWISE:
   913	            key += ":" + chess_stepwise.STEPWISE
   914	        return key
   915	
   916	    def trial_identity(trial: int) -> str:
   917	        # The ordinal repeats in each round and harness revision within an outer run.
   918	        return f"round-{round_no}.harness-{harness_sha}.trial-{trial + 1}"
   919	
   920	    cache = cache if cache is not None else {}
   921	    per_task, traces, verdicts = {}, {}, {}
   922	    per_task_scorer_isolation = {}
   923	    #: One score per trial, per task, kept so a reader can see the spread rather than
   924	    #: only its mean. Absent when `trials == 1`.
   925	    per_task_trials: dict[str, list[float]] = {}
   926	    per_task_std: dict[str, float] = {}
   927	    #: **Every** trial's trace and verdict, so a task that passed twice out of three can
   928	    #: be explained (`traces`/`verdicts` keep only the last trial, for compatibility).
   929	    #: Absent when `trials == 1`, where they would repeat the two dicts above.
   930	    trial_traces: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
   931	    trial_verdicts: dict[str, list[dict]] = {}
   932	    #: Tasks whose measurement was **reused** rather than taken, by task id. Recorded on
   933	    #: the point because a reused measurement is not an independent one: with the cache
   934	    #: content-addressed (see `driver.py`), an unchanged tree has exactly one measurement,
   935	    #: and a reader who sees the same number twice must be able to tell that from two
   936	    #: measurements that happened to agree.
   937	    cached_tasks: list[str] = []
   938	    #: Tasks the platform could not measure, as opposed to ones it measured as zero.
   939	    #: Kept out of `per_task` entirely (INTERFACE.md §2.5.7): a task that never ran is
   940	    #: not a task that scored nothing, and putting a `0.0` here is the specific lie
   941	    #: this whole mechanism exists to prevent.
   942	    invalid: dict[str, dict] = {}
   943	    #: Tasks the round **stopped short of**, because the harness had already failed to
   944	    #: start on every task it had tried. Kept out of `per_task` for the same reason
   945	    #: `invalid` is: those tasks were never measured, and a `0.0` for them would be the
   946	    #: platform's invention rather than its verdict.
   947	    not_run: list[str] = []
   948	    #: Tasks the platform *did* measure but whose harness left no trace, with the exit
   949	    #: code and the tail of its stderr. Kept apart from `invalid` on purpose: an invalid
   950	    #: task is absent from `per_task` because there is no measurement, while these keep
   951	    #: their score. Same split the platform makes everywhere else -- the attribution is
   952	    #: not a reason to throw the number away.
   953	    harness_failed: dict[str, dict] = {}
   954	    #: What each harness **printed**, per task, kept whether it failed or not.
   955	    #:
   956	    #: `harness_failed` is the narrow version of this: it fires only when the trace is
   957	    #: empty, and it answers "did the harness start?". A task that scored 0 with a
   958	    #: non-empty trace still has a question attached to it ("it ran, so what did it
   959	    #: say?"), and the answer was in `result["stderr"]` and thrown away. Measured: four
   960	    #: of five tasks at 0.0 on `loop-terminal_bench-26452c` with no explanation anywhere
   961	    #: in the run; the explanation had to be reconstructed by hand from the traces.
   962	    harness_output: dict[str, dict] = {}
   963	    #: The check's own tally, as a rate per task. **A second, finer signal beside
   964	    #: `score`.** A `command` check usually reports "4 passed 1 failed" and the reward file
   965	    #: collapses that to 0. Measured on this platform: a task that passed 4 of 5 tests
   966	    #: scored 0.0, and a harness edit that took it to 3 of 5 also scored 0.0 -- the curve
   967	    #: said "no change" while the artifact got *worse*, and a task set of ten binary scores
   968	    #: (resolution 0.1, noise +/-0.1) cannot resolve the effect the platform exists to
   969	    #: measure. `score` stays the headline and stays comparable; this is the same
   970	    #: measurement at the resolution the check already provides.
   971	    #:
   972	    #: Only tasks whose check reports tests appear, so `test_rate_n_tasks` travels beside
   973	    #: the mean -- a 0.8 over three tasks and a 0.8 over ten are not the same claim.
   974	    per_task_test_rate: dict[str, float] = {}
   975	    per_task_test_rate_trials: dict[str, list[float]] = {}
   976	    tests_passed_total = 0
   977	    tests_total_total = 0
   978	    #: Which interpreter ran the harness on each task and where its own dependencies
   979	    #: came from (`eval/containers/harness_runtime.py`). Distinct records, because the version is a
   980	    #: property of the task's image and one run may cross several.
   981	    runtime_records: list[dict] = []
   982	    declarations: list[dict] = []
   983	    tampered: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
   984	    env_usage = {"input": 0, "output": 0, "calls": 0, "services_reported": 0}
   985	    #: Whether the platform had to publish the agent's model onto the task's network.
   986	    #: A fact about the measurement: a containerised harness that could not reach a
   987	    #: model would score zero for a reason that is not about the harness.
   988	    model_gateway: dict | None = None
   989	
   990	    # The harness is arbitrary code the platform did not write, and it runs with
   991	    # the platform on disk. One pair of hashes around the whole task set, not one
   992	    # pair per task: a 400-task benchmark would otherwise hash the platform 400
   993	    # times, and the cost of the check would scale with the cost of the run it is
   994	    # protecting. Granularity is not lost -- the report names files, and which
   995	    # task touched them is not something a failing run needs.
   996	    before = snapshot(PLATFORM_ROOT)
   997	
   998	    # ---- one task, `trials` times, as a function ----------------------------------
   999	    #
  1000	    # Extracted from the loop this used to be so that `--jobs N` can measure several tasks
  1001	    # at once, and the extraction is what makes that safe: this function mutates **nothing**
  1002	    # the other tasks share. It returns what it measured and the merge below writes it,
  1003	    # whereas the loop it came from wrote into eight shared dicts as it went. Two threads
  1004	    # assigning different keys of one dict is not a race, but the *order* of two writes is,
  1005	    # and a curve that pairs one task's score with another task's trace is exactly the kind
  1006	    # of number this platform exists to refuse.
  1007	    def _measure(task_index: int, task: dict) -> dict:
  1008	        tid = task["task_id"]
  1009	        enforcement = (diagnostic_authorization.record()
  1010	                       if diagnostic_authorization is not None else None)
  1011	        # Cache entries carry scores, not fresh backend enforcement observations.
  1012	        refusal = _refuse_unobserved_policies(task, (envs or {}).get(tid),
  1013	                                              (verifiers or {}).get(tid), run_id=run_id,
  1014	                                              diagnostic_authorization=diagnostic_authorization)
  1015	        if refusal is not None:
  1016	            _write_progress(progress_dir, round_no, tid, [], refusal["invalid"],
  1017	                            diagnostic=refusal, enforcement=enforcement)
  1018	            return {"task_id": tid, "invalid": refusal["invalid"]}
  1019	        # A cache hit cannot bypass the interactive isolation/input requirements.
  1020	        # Construction is local and lazy; it neither loads the CSV nor launches I/O.
  1021	        if evaluation_protocols.get(tid) == chess_stepwise.STEPWISE:
  1022	            try:
  1023	                chess_stepwise.session_for(task, (verifiers or {}).get(tid),
  1024	                                           sandbox=sandbox, env=(envs or {}).get(tid) or {})
  1025	            except ValueError as exc:
  1026	                refusal = _invalid(task, exc, stage="stepwise admission", service=None)
  1027	                _write_progress(progress_dir, round_no, tid, [], refusal["invalid"],
  1028	                                diagnostic=refusal)
  1029	                return {"task_id": tid, "invalid": refusal["invalid"]}
  1030	        # The trial count is part of the key: a cached single measurement is not a
  1031	        # cached mean of three, and reusing one for the other would quietly answer a
  1032	        # different question than the run asked.
  1033	        key = measurement_key(task)
  1034	        hit = cache.get(key)
  1035	        if hit is not None:
  1036	            if declared_class((verifiers or {}).get(tid)) == "pristine-offline":
  1037	                cached = hit.get("trial_verdicts") or [hit.get("verdict") or {}]
  1038	                if len(cached) != trials:
  1039	                    return {"task_id": tid, "invalid": {"stage": "check",
  1040	                            "detail": "pristine cached verdict execution binding coverage mismatch"}}
  1041	                for index, verdict in enumerate(cached):
  1042	                    checked = enforce_verdict_binding(verdict, task_id=tid, run_id=run_id,
  1043	                                                      trial_id=trial_identity(index))
  1044	                    if checked.get("unmeasured"):
  1045	                        return {"task_id": tid, "invalid": {"stage": "check",
  1046	                                "detail": checked["unmeasured"]}}
  1047	            return {"task_id": tid, "cached": hit}
  1048	
  1049	        # One task, one record, and the phases inside it belong to it. Where this
  1050	        # context is set, `eval/containers/container.py` starts beating while it waits, so a
  1051	        # task that spends 40 minutes inside a single `docker exec` stops being
  1052	        # indistinguishable from a dead driver. See `eval/observe/progress.py`.
  1053	        # `round_no` is what makes the event stream readable: every phase of every task
  1054	        # used to be labelled round 0, including the candidate rounds, so a log could not
  1055	        # say which round a task belonged to. The traces and verdicts were stored per
  1056	        # round all along; only the live record had lost the number.
  1057	        # One task, `trials` times. See `evaluate` for why one is not enough.
  1058	        measured: list[dict] = []
  1059	        unmeasured: dict | None = None
  1060	        for trial in range(trials):
  1061	            with progress_mod.task_progress(tid, round_no=round_no, index=task_index,
  1062	                                            total=len(tasks)) as held:
  1063	                try:
  1064	                    result = run_one(repo, task, sandbox=sandbox,
  1065	                                     setup=(setups or {}).get(tid),
  1066	                                     verifier=(verifiers or {}).get(tid),
  1067	                                     env=(envs or {}).get(tid),
  1068	                                     run_id=run_id, run_seed=run_seed,
  1069	                                     recordings_root=recordings_root,
  1070	                                     **({"trial_id": trial_identity(trial)}
  1071	                                        if declared_class((verifiers or {}).get(tid)) == "pristine-offline" else {}),
  1072	                                     **({"diagnostic_authorization": diagnostic_authorization}
  1073	                                        if diagnostic_authorization is not None else {}))
  1074	                except BaseException as exc:
  1075	                    # The record says the task died and why; the exception still travels.
  1076	                    held.failed(f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}")
  1077	                    raise
  1078	
  1079	                # A task the platform could not measure never reaches scoring. It is *not*
  1080	                # cached either: the cache key names the harness, and the reason this
  1081	                # task failed was not the harness, so caching it would carry a dataset bug
  1082	                # into every later round and hide the round where it was introduced.
  1083	                if result.get("invalid"):
  1084	                    unmeasured = result["invalid"]
  1085	                    held.failed(str(unmeasured.get("detail") or "")[:200])
  1086	                    break
  1087	                if declared_class((verifiers or {}).get(tid)) == "pristine-offline":
  1088	                    result["verdict"] = enforce_verdict_binding(
  1089	                        result.get("verdict") or {}, task_id=tid, run_id=run_id,
  1090	                        trial_id=trial_identity(trial))
  1091	                # **The harness died because it could not reach the model endpoint.**
  1092	                # `_provider_failure` has existed since the bug it describes was measured
  1093	                # and was, until this call, only ever exercised by its own tests -- so a run
  1094	                # in which 20 of 91 task-runs died on `APIConnectionError` recorded them as
  1095	                # ordinary non-zero exits, and the curve read a gateway outage as 20 tasks
  1096	                # the harness could not do. Three guard rails, each measured:
  1097	                #
  1098	                #   * a `passed` verdict is kept, because a harness that crashed *after*
  1099	                #     writing the right artifact finished the task (INTERFACE.md §2.2);
  1100	                #   * a trial that already produced a verdict is kept: the endpoint is
  1101	                #     exactly the noise `--trials` exists to average, and discarding the one
  1102	                #     good sample to report a blip is the wrong trade;
  1103	                #   * the gateway's own counts ride along in `detail`, so the record says
  1104	                #     *who* hung up rather than only that a connection failed.
  1105	                provider = _provider_failure(result)
  1106	                if (provider is not None and not (result.get("verdict") or {}).get("passed")
  1107	                        and (result.get("verdict") or {}).get("protocol") != chess_stepwise.STEPWISE):
  1108	                    if measured:
  1109	                        print(f"warning: {tid}: trial {trial + 1} died reaching the model "
  1110	                              f"endpoint ({provider['line']}); keeping the "
  1111	                              f"{len(measured)} measured trial(s)", file=sys.stderr)
  1112	                        continue
  1113	                    unmeasured = {
  1114	                        "stage": "harness model call",
  1115	                        "detail": (f"the harness could not reach the model endpoint and "
  1116	                                   f"exited {provider['exit_code']}: {provider['line']}")
  1117	                                  + modelgate_mod.describe(result.get("model_gateway"))}
  1118	                    held.failed(str(unmeasured["detail"])[:200])
  1119	                    break
  1120	                # **A check that never ran is not a zero.** The platform can prove it when the
  1121	                # check was supposed to leave a machine-readable report and did not
  1122	                # (`_verify_container_state` marks the verdict): then nothing tested the
  1123	                # artifact, and recording 0.0 would blame the harness for a task nobody
  1124	                # measured. Same distinction §2.5.7 draws for a task the platform could not
  1125	                # start -- this is the check's own half of it.
  1126	                if (result.get("verdict") or {}).get("unmeasured"):
  1127	                    unmeasured = {"stage": "check",
  1128	                                  "detail": (result["verdict"] or {})["unmeasured"]}
  1129	                    if result["verdict"].get("outcome"):
  1130	                        unmeasured["outcome"] = result["verdict"]["outcome"]
  1131	                    if result["verdict"].get("protocol"):
  1132	                        unmeasured.update(protocol=result["verdict"]["protocol"],
  1133	                                          receipt=result["verdict"].get("evidence"))
  1134	                    held.failed(str(unmeasured["detail"])[:200])
  1135	                    break
  1136	
  1137	                # The score the platform computed for this task. Set on the holder so the
  1138	                # task's closing record carries it: a panel showing "task 3/5 done" without
  1139	                # the number is a progress bar; with it, it is the round forming.
  1140	                held.score = float((result.get("verdict") or {}).get(
  1141	                    "score", 1.0 if (result.get("verdict") or {}).get("passed") else 0.0))
  1142	
  1143	            measured.append(result)
  1144	
  1145	        if unmeasured is not None:
  1146	            # Not cached, and deliberately: the cache key names the harness, and the reason
  1147	            # this task failed was not the harness, so caching it would carry a dataset bug
  1148	            # into every later round and hide the round where it was introduced.
  1149	            _write_progress(progress_dir, round_no, tid, [], unmeasured,
  1150	                            diagnostic=result, enforcement=enforcement)
  1151	            return {"task_id": tid, "invalid": unmeasured}
  1152	        _write_progress(progress_dir, round_no, tid, measured, None, enforcement=enforcement)
  1153	        return {"task_id": tid, "measured": measured}
  1154	
  1155	    outcomes = _in_parallel(tasks, _measure, jobs)
  1156	
  1157	    # The merge. Sequential and in **task order** on purpose: a run that used `--jobs 3`
  1158	    # and one that did not must produce comparable records, down to the order of
  1159	    # `runtime_records` and `declarations` -- an order decided by whichever thread finished
  1160	    # first would not be.
  1161	    for task_index, task in enumerate(tasks, start=1):
  1162	        tid = task["task_id"]
  1163	        key = measurement_key(task)
  1164	        outcome = outcomes[tid]
  1165	
  1166	        # The round stopped before this task: the harness had already failed to start on
  1167	        # `jobs` others, so there is no measurement here to keep and none to invent.
  1168	        if outcome.get("not_run"):
  1169	            not_run.append(tid)
  1170	            continue
  1171	
  1172	        # A cached task never ran: its records are copied back exactly as they were stored.
  1173	        if "cached" in outcome:
  1174	            entry = outcome["cached"]
  1175	            cached_tasks.append(tid)
  1176	            per_task[tid] = entry["score"]
  1177	            cached_verdicts = entry.get("trial_verdicts") or [entry.get("verdict") or {}]
  1178	            per_task_scorer_isolation[tid] = common_class(observed_class(v) for v in cached_verdicts)
  1179	            if entry.get("per_task_trials"):
  1180	                per_task_trials[tid] = list(entry["per_task_trials"])
  1181	                per_task_std[tid] = float(entry.get("per_task_std") or 0.0)
  1182	            if entry.get("trial_traces"):
  1183	                trial_traces[tid] = list(entry["trial_traces"])
  1184	            if entry.get("trial_verdicts"):
  1185	                trial_verdicts[tid] = [dict(v) for v in entry["trial_verdicts"]]
  1186	                for v in trial_verdicts[tid]:
  1187	                    v.setdefault("scorer_isolation_class", "unknown")
  1188	            traces[tid] = entry["trace"]
  1189	            if entry.get("harness_failed"):
  1190	                harness_failed[tid] = entry["harness_failed"]
  1191	            cached_record = entry.get("harness_runtime")
  1192	            if cached_record and cached_record not in runtime_records:
  1193	                runtime_records.append(cached_record)
  1194	            if entry.get("harness_output"):
  1195	                harness_output[tid] = entry["harness_output"]
  1196	            if entry.get("verdict"):
  1197	                verdicts[tid] = dict(entry["verdict"])
  1198	                verdicts[tid].setdefault("scorer_isolation_class", "unknown")
  1199	                if tid in evaluation_protocols:
  1200	                    verdicts[tid]["protocol"] = evaluation_protocols[tid]
  1201	            continue
  1202	
  1203	        if outcome.get("invalid"):
  1204	            invalid[tid] = outcome["invalid"]
  1205	            continue
  1206	
  1207	        measured = outcome["measured"]
  1208	        # The records a reader inspects (trace, verdict, harness output) come from the
  1209	        # **last** trial when there is more than one; only one of them can be on disk, and
  1210	        # saying which is the whole of the rule.
  1211	        result = measured[-1]
  1212	        # The score is the *platform's* verdict on what the harness left behind, and
  1213	        # the harness's exit code deliberately does not enter it: a run that crashed
  1214	        # but produced a working artifact completed the task. See INTERFACE.md §2.2.
  1215	        verdict = result.get("verdict") or {}
  1216	
  1217	        def _score_of(res: dict) -> float:
  1218	            """`score` first: a `reward_file` check returns a number and the platform must
  1219	            use it, not collapse it to "did it pass". A fractional reward is a score
  1220	            (INTERFACE.md §2.5.9), and discarding it would silently turn a 0.5 into a 1.
  1221	            """
  1222	            v = res.get("verdict") or {}
  1223	            return float(v.get("score", 1.0 if v.get("passed") else 0.0))
  1224	
  1225	        scores = [_score_of(res) for res in measured]
  1226	        per_task_scorer_isolation[tid] = common_class(
  1227	            observed_class(res.get("verdict")) for res in measured)
  1228	        score = sum(scores) / len(scores)
  1229	        if trials > 1:
  1230	            per_task_trials[tid] = scores
  1231	            per_task_std[tid] = _spread(scores)
  1232	        per_task[tid] = score
  1233	        # The same trials, read for the check's own tally instead of its reward. Kept
  1234	        # beside the score rather than replacing it: `score` is what the task is worth and
  1235	        # is what every existing reader and every cross-run comparison uses.
  1236	        rates = [test_rate_of(res.get("verdict")) for res in measured]
  1237	        rates = [r for r in rates if r is not None]
  1238	        if rates:
  1239	            per_task_test_rate[tid] = sum(rates) / len(rates)
  1240	            if trials > 1:
  1241	                per_task_test_rate_trials[tid] = rates
  1242	            for res in measured:
  1243	                tally = (res.get("verdict") or {}).get("tests") or {}
  1244	                passed_n, failed_n = tally.get("passed"), tally.get("failed")
  1245	                if isinstance(passed_n, int) and isinstance(failed_n, int):
  1246	                    tests_passed_total += passed_n
  1247	                    tests_total_total += passed_n + failed_n
  1248	        verdicts[tid] = verdict
  1249	        traces[tid] = result["trace"]
  1250	        # **Every trial, not only the last one.** Measured on `B2-noop` (2026-10-04):
  1251	        # `cobol-modernization` scored 0.667 -- two of three trials passed -- and the
  1252	        # saved verdict was the *last* trial's, which passed. So the record said
  1253	        # "passed" beside a score of 0.667, and the failing trial's trace and reason
  1254	        # were nowhere: the one question `--trials` exists to answer ("it passed twice
  1255	        # out of three -- what happened the third time?") had no answer. Worse, the
  1256	        # same artifact scored 2/3 in one measurement and 3/3 in the next with a
  1257	        # byte-identical trajectory, which is a statement about the *check* rather than
  1258	        # the harness, and it cannot be checked at all without the other trials.
  1259	        #
  1260	        # Kept as a list beside `traces`/`verdicts` (which stay the last trial, so
  1261	        # every existing reader keeps working) rather than replacing them.
  1262	        if trials > 1:
  1263	            trial_traces[tid] = [res["trace"] for res in measured]
  1264	            trial_verdicts[tid] = [dict(res.get("verdict") or {}) for res in measured]
  1265	        # A harness that left no trace is recorded next to its score rather than
  1266	        # instead of it. See `_harness_failure` for what this looked like when it was
  1267	        # dropped: a crashed harness and a harness that chose not to edit were the same
  1268	        # reading, and the run said nothing either way.
  1269	        failure = _harness_failure(result)
  1270	        # Both ends. A tail alone lost the harness's own prints on the one run that
  1271	        # needed them most: measured on an eval run of `sanitize-git-repo`, 6 KB of
  1272	        # `openai` retry traceback filled the capture completely and the first thing the
  1273	        # harness said was gone. The head is where a harness states what it is doing.
  1274	        raw = result.get("stderr") or ""
  1275	        harness_output[tid] = {"exit_code": result.get("exit_code"),
  1276	                               "stderr": (raw if len(raw) <= 8000
  1277	                                          else raw[:2000] + "\n...\n" + raw[-6000:])}
  1278	        if failure:
  1279	            harness_failed[tid] = failure
  1280	        runtime_record = result.get("harness_runtime")
  1281	        if runtime_record and runtime_record not in runtime_records:
  1282	            runtime_records.append(runtime_record)
  1283	        for key_name in ("input", "output", "calls"):
  1284	            value = (result.get("env_usage") or {}).get(
  1285	                {"input": "input_tokens", "output": "output_tokens",
  1286	                 "calls": "calls"}[key_name])
  1287	            if isinstance(value, int):
  1288	                env_usage[key_name] += value
  1289	        env_usage["services_reported"] += (result.get("env_usage") or {}).get(
  1290	            "services_reported", 0)
  1291	        if result.get("model_gateway"):
  1292	            model_gateway = result["model_gateway"]
  1293	        declared = result.get("identity")
  1294	        if declared:
  1295	            declarations.append(declared)
  1296	        cache[key] = {"score": score, "trace": result["trace"],
  1297	                      "per_task_trials": per_task_trials.get(tid),
  1298	                      "per_task_std": per_task_std.get(tid),
  1299	                      # The other trials travel with the score too. A cached round used to
  1300	                      # restore only the last trial's trace and verdict, so the round that
  1301	                      # *reused* a measurement was exactly the round whose 2/3 could not be
  1302	                      # explained.
  1303	                      "trial_traces": trial_traces.get(tid),
  1304	                      "trial_verdicts": trial_verdicts.get(tid),
  1305	                      "harness_failed": failure,
  1306	                      "harness_output": harness_output.get(tid),
  1307	                      # The verdict travels with the score. Without this, a cached round
  1308	                      # writes `score 0.0, kind null, detail ""` -- the exact "a number
  1309	                      # with no explanation" this record exists to prevent. Measured on
  1310	                      # `loop-terminal_bench-41133` round 5: all five verdict files were
  1311	                      # empty because the round was entirely cache hits.
  1312	                      "verdict": verdicts.get(tid) or {},
  1313	                      "harness_runtime": result.get("harness_runtime")}
  1314	
  1315	    # What the harness spent. Read from the traces rather than asked for: a harness
  1316	    # that reports its own cost can under-report, and every harness already writes
  1317	    # `usage` into its trace because the contributor spec requires it. A method's
  1318	    # acceptance rule may be cost-aware (RRSI's is: it permits a candidate to spend
  1319	    # more tokens only in proportion to the score it gains), and a cost rule fed a
  1320	    # constant zero is not a weak rule, it is no rule -- measured: the platform
  1321	    # reported `generation_tokens: 0` for every point, so `Delta C` was always 0 and
  1322	    # `beta0 + beta1 * Delta S` was satisfied by every candidate.
  1323	    tokens = {"input": 0, "output": 0, "calls": 0, "tasks_reported": 0}
  1324	    for trace in traces.values():
  1325	        usage = _last_usage(trace)
  1326	        if usage is None:
  1327	            continue
  1328	        tokens["tasks_reported"] += 1
  1329	        for key, field in (("input", "input_tokens"), ("output", "output_tokens")):
  1330	            value = usage.get(field)
  1331	            if isinstance(value, int):
  1332	                tokens[key] += value
  1333	        if isinstance(usage.get("calls"), int):
  1334	            tokens["calls"] += usage["calls"]
  1335	
  1336	    # A cached task never ran, so a fully-cached evaluation reports nothing --
  1337	    # correct: the platform did not execute anything this time.
  1338	    tampered = {k: v for k, v in diff(before, snapshot(PLATFORM_ROOT)).items() if v}
  1339	    # What the harness said it was. One declaration is the normal case; more than one
  1340	    # distinct one means the harness changed what ran it mid-run, which is a fact a
  1341	    # reader needs rather than something to average away.
  1342	    identity_declared = None
  1343	    identity_variants: list[dict] = []
  1344	    for declared in declarations:
  1345	        if declared not in identity_variants:
  1346	            identity_variants.append(declared)
  1347	    if identity_variants:
  1348	        identity_declared = identity_variants[0]
  1349	
  1350	    #: The spread of the *run-level* score across trials: per trial, the mean over the
  1351	    #: tasks measured in it. A reader comparing two harnesses needs this number, not the
  1352	    #: per-task one, because the curve is drawn from run-level scores.
  1353	    scorer_isolation_class = common_class(per_task_scorer_isolation.values())
  1354	    score_std = 0.0
  1355	    if trials > 1 and per_task_trials:
  1356	        width = min(len(v) for v in per_task_trials.values())
  1357	        run_scores = [sum(v[i] for v in per_task_trials.values()) / len(per_task_trials)
  1358	                      for i in range(width)]
  1359	        score_std = _spread(run_scores)
  1360	
  1361	    # **A round whose harness never started anywhere is not a weak harness.** The per-task
  1362	    # facts were already recorded (`harness_failed`) and warned about one by one; what was
  1363	    # missing is the round-level sentence, so a candidate that never ran once reached the
  1364	    # curve as an ordinary `0.000` -- the same number as one that ran and lost. Those two
  1365	    # readings call for different decisions (a weak harness is a result; a harness that
  1366	    # cannot start is a candidate to reject), and `codex-dgm` produced the first while
  1367	    # reading as the second for two rounds running.
  1368	    #
  1369	    # The score is kept rather than dropped: those tasks *were* measured, the platform's
  1370	    # verdict on what the harness left behind stands, and a method that broke the artifact
  1371	    # should see it in its own history. What changes is that the point now names which of
  1372	    # the two it is.
  1373	    startup_failure = None
  1374	    if harness_failed and not (set(per_task) - set(harness_failed)):
  1375	        first = sorted(harness_failed)[0]
  1376	        why = harness_failed[first] or {}
  1377	        startup_failure = {
  1378	            "tasks": len(harness_failed),
  1379	            "first_task": first,
  1380	            "reason": why.get("reason"),
  1381	            "exit_code": why.get("exit_code"),
  1382	            "stderr_tail": " ".join((why.get("stderr") or "").split())[-400:],
  1383	            "not_run": sorted(not_run),
  1384	        }
  1385	    return {"per_task": per_task, "per_task_std": per_task_std, "score_std": score_std,
  1386	            "scorer_isolation_class": scorer_isolation_class,
  1387	            # Preserve unobserved diagnostic facts without making them comparable.
  1388	            "scorer_isolation_comparable": scorer_isolation_class != "unknown",
  1389	            "per_task_scorer_isolation": per_task_scorer_isolation,
  1390	            "per_task_trials": per_task_trials, "trials": trials,
  1391	            # The same measurement at the check's own resolution. `test_rate` averages only
  1392	            # the tasks whose check reports a tally, so `test_rate_n_tasks` travels with it.
  1393	            "per_task_test_rate": per_task_test_rate,
  1394	            "per_task_test_rate_trials": per_task_test_rate_trials,
  1395	            "test_rate": (sum(per_task_test_rate.values()) / len(per_task_test_rate)
  1396	                          if per_task_test_rate else None),
  1397	            "test_rate_n_tasks": len(per_task_test_rate),
  1398	            "tests_passed": tests_passed_total,
  1399	            "tests_total": tests_total_total,
  1400	            "traces": traces, "tampered": tampered,
  1401	            "trial_traces": trial_traces, "trial_verdicts": trial_verdicts,
  1402	            "cached_tasks": cached_tasks,
  1403	            "verdicts": verdicts,
  1404	            "evaluation_protocols": evaluation_protocols,
  1405	            "invalid": invalid,
  1406	            "not_run": not_run,
  1407	            "startup_failure": startup_failure,
  1408	            "harness_failed": harness_failed,
  1409	            "harness_output": harness_output,
  1410	            "harness_runtime": runtime_records,
  1411	            "env_usage": env_usage,
  1412	            "model_gateway": model_gateway,
  1413	            "identity_declared": identity_declared,
  1414	            "identity_variants": identity_variants,
  1415	            "tokens": tokens}

$ nl -ba eval/admission/lifecycle_receipt.py
     1	"""Pure, immutable lifecycle receipts for trusted orchestration.
     2	
     3	No execution, observation collection, deletion, lease updates or authorization.
     4	Digests bind supplied evidence; they do not authenticate its origin. Backends
     5	must collect facts independently of the actor and persist each returned receipt.
     6	"""
     7	from __future__ import annotations
     8	
     9	import copy
    10	import hashlib
    11	import json
    12	
    13	from eval.admission import runner_admission as admission
    14	
    15	SCHEMA = "harnessgrad.lifecycle-receipt.v1"
    16	ATTESTATION_SCHEMA = "harnessgrad.lifecycle-attestation.v1"
    17	STAGES = ("scope", "admit", "execute", "quiesce", "score", "seal", "cleanup")
    18	MAX_BYTES = 128 * 1024
    19	
    20	
    21	def digest(value) -> str:
    22	    return hashlib.sha256(json.dumps(value, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"),
    23	                                    allow_nan=False).encode()).hexdigest()
    24	
    25	
    26	def _require(condition, message):
    27	    if not condition:
    28	        raise ValueError(message)
    29	
    30	
    31	def _bounded(value):
    32	    _require(len(json.dumps(value, allow_nan=False).encode()) <= MAX_BYTES,
    33	             "lifecycle receipt too large")
    34	
    35	
    36	def start_lifecycle(binding: dict, scope_sha256: str) -> dict:
    37	    """Freeze scope before admission; scope hash names the pre-execution plan."""
    38	    admission._binding(binding)
    39	    admission._sha(scope_sha256, "invalid_scope_hash")
    40	    return dict(schema=SCHEMA, binding=copy.deepcopy(binding), scope_sha256=scope_sha256,
    41	                state="scope", status="passed", events=[], publication_ready=False,
    42	                production_authorized=False)
    43	
    44	
    45	def _gate(binding, stage, evidence):
    46	    # Recheck original supplied facts with the shared gate, rather than trusting a
    47	    # free-standing status boolean or an adapter-provided admission receipt.
    48	    if isinstance(evidence, dict) and set(evidence) == {"refusal"}:
    49	        # A persisted refusal can only deny progress. Never persist rejected raw
    50	        # input, which may include task payloads, private logs or credentials.
    51	        receipt = evidence["refusal"]
    52	        keys = {"schema", "stage", "status", "measured", "production_authorized", "binding", "reason"}
    53	        if stage == "cleanup":
    54	            keys.add("lease_action")
    55	        _require(isinstance(receipt, dict) and keys <= set(receipt)
    56	                 and set(receipt) <= keys | {"policy_sha256", "observation_sha256"}
    57	                 and receipt["schema"] == "harnessgrad.runner-admission.v1"
    58	                 and receipt["stage"] == stage and receipt["status"] == "blocked"
    59	                 and receipt["measured"] is False and receipt["production_authorized"] is False
    60	                 and admission._binding(receipt["binding"]) == binding
    61	                 and (stage != "cleanup" or receipt["lease_action"] == "quarantine"),
    62	                 "invalid persisted admission refusal")
    63	        admission._text(receipt["reason"], "invalid_refusal_reason")
    64	        for key in ("policy_sha256", "observation_sha256"):
    65	            if key in receipt:
    66	                admission._sha(receipt[key], "invalid_refusal_hash")
    67	        return receipt
    68	    _require(isinstance(evidence, dict) and set(evidence) == {"policy", "observed"},
    69	             "admission evidence requires policy and observed facts")
    70	    try:
    71	        return getattr(admission, "admit_" + stage)(binding, **evidence)
    72	    except admission.AdmissionError as exc:
    73	        _require(exc.receipt is not None, "missing admission refusal receipt")
    74	        return exc.receipt
    75	
    76	
    77	def _attestation(binding, stage, evidence):
    78	    _require(isinstance(evidence, dict) and set(evidence) == {
    79	        "schema", "binding", "stage", "status", "receipt_sha256", "facts", "detail"},
    80	        "invalid lifecycle attestation")
    81	    _require(evidence["schema"] == ATTESTATION_SCHEMA and evidence["stage"] == stage,
    82	             "wrong lifecycle attestation stage")
    83	    _require(admission._binding(evidence["binding"]) == binding, "stale lifecycle attestation")
    84	    _require(evidence["status"] in ("passed", "failed", "unknown"), "invalid attestation status")
    85	    admission._text(evidence["detail"], "invalid_attestation_detail")
    86	    _require(isinstance(evidence["facts"], dict), "invalid attestation facts")
    87	    if evidence["status"] != "passed":
    88	        _require(evidence["facts"] == {}, "failed/unknown attestation must not claim passing facts")
    89	        if evidence["receipt_sha256"] is not None:
    90	            admission._sha(evidence["receipt_sha256"], "invalid_attestation_hash")
    91	        return evidence
    92	    admission._sha(evidence["receipt_sha256"], "invalid_attestation_hash")
    93	    facts = evidence["facts"]
    94	    if stage in ("execute", "quiesce"):
    95	        keys = {"exit_code", "oom_killed", "timed_out"}
    96	        if stage == "quiesce":
    97	            keys.add("running")
    98	        _require(set(facts) == keys and type(facts["exit_code"]) is int
    99	                 and facts["exit_code"] == 0 and facts["oom_killed"] is False
   100	                 and facts["timed_out"] is False
   101	                 and (stage != "quiesce" or facts["running"] is False),
   102	                 "successful execution/quiescence unproven")
   103	    elif stage == "score":
   104	        _require(set(facts) == {"outcomes_sha256"}, "invalid scoring attestation")
   105	        admission._sha(facts["outcomes_sha256"], "invalid_outcomes_hash")
   106	    elif stage == "seal":
   107	        _require(set(facts) == {"outcomes_sha256", "snapshot"}, "invalid seal attestation")
   108	        admission._sha(facts["outcomes_sha256"], "invalid_outcomes_hash")
   109	        snapshot = facts["snapshot"]
   110	        _attestation(binding, "snapshot", snapshot)
   111	        _require(snapshot["status"] == "passed", "snapshot completion must pass before seal")
   112	    elif stage == "snapshot":
   113	        # A preflight receipt alone cannot attest completed snapshot/export work.
   114	        _require(set(facts) == {"completed", "artifact_sha256", "exit_code", "oom_killed",
   115	                               "timed_out", "preflight", "runtime"}, "invalid snapshot completion attestation")
   116	        _require(facts["completed"] is True and type(facts["exit_code"]) is int
   117	                 and facts["exit_code"] == 0 and facts["oom_killed"] is False
   118	                 and facts["timed_out"] is False, "snapshot completion unproven")
   119	        admission._sha(facts["artifact_sha256"], "invalid_snapshot_artifact_hash")
   120	        _require(_gate(binding, "snapshot", facts["preflight"])["status"] == "passed",
   121	                 "snapshot preflight must pass")
   122	        _require(_gate(binding, "runtime", facts["runtime"])["status"] == "passed",
   123	                 "snapshot worker runtime admission must pass")
   124	    return evidence
   125	
   126	
   127	def _advance(receipt, stage, evidence):
   128	    _require(stage in STAGES[1:], "unknown lifecycle stage")
   129	    _require(receipt["state"] != "cleanup", "cleanup is terminal; use a fresh run ID")
   130	    if receipt["status"] != "passed":
   131	        _require(stage == "cleanup", "failed lifecycle may only attempt cleanup")
   132	    else:
   133	        _require(stage == STAGES[STAGES.index(receipt["state"]) + 1],
   134	                 "lifecycle stage skipped or repeated")
   135	    if stage in ("admit", "cleanup"):
   136	        checked = _gate(receipt["binding"], "runtime" if stage == "admit" else stage, evidence)
   137	        status = "passed" if checked["status"] == "passed" else "failed"
   138	        if status == "failed":
   139	            evidence = {"refusal": checked}
   140	    else:
   141	        checked = _attestation(receipt["binding"], stage, evidence)
   142	        status = checked["status"]
   143	    if stage == "seal" and status == "passed":
   144	        scored = receipt["events"][-1]["evidence"]["facts"]["outcomes_sha256"]
   145	        _require(checked["facts"]["outcomes_sha256"] == scored, "sealed outcomes differ from scoring")
   146	    result = copy.deepcopy(receipt)
   147	    result["events"].append(dict(stage=stage, status=status,
   148	                                 evidence=copy.deepcopy(evidence), receipt_sha256=digest(checked)))
   149	    result["state"] = stage
   150	    # Failed/unknown work remains terminal for scoring even after successful cleanup.
   151	    result["status"] = status if receipt["status"] == "passed" else receipt["status"]
   152	    result["publication_ready"] = stage == "cleanup" and result["status"] == "passed"
   153	    _bounded(result)
   154	    return result
   155	
   156	
   157	def validate_lifecycle_receipt(receipt: dict) -> None:
   158	    """Replay checks without trusting derived states, statuses or event digests."""
   159	    _require(isinstance(receipt, dict) and set(receipt) == {
   160	        "schema", "binding", "scope_sha256", "state", "status", "events",
   161	        "publication_ready", "production_authorized"} and receipt["schema"] == SCHEMA,
   162	        "unsupported lifecycle receipt")
   163	    _bounded(receipt)
   164	    _require(isinstance(receipt["events"], list) and len(receipt["events"]) <= 6,
   165	             "invalid lifecycle event count")
   166	    replay = start_lifecycle(receipt["binding"], receipt["scope_sha256"])
   167	    for event in receipt["events"]:
   168	        _require(isinstance(event, dict) and set(event) == {
   169	            "stage", "status", "evidence", "receipt_sha256"}, "invalid lifecycle event")
   170	        replay = _advance(replay, event["stage"], event["evidence"])
   171	    _require(replay == receipt and type(receipt["publication_ready"]) is bool
   172	             and receipt["production_authorized"] is False, "inconsistent lifecycle receipt")
   173	
   174	
   175	def transition(receipt: dict, stage: str, evidence: dict) -> dict:
   176	    """Return a new checkpoint. No skipped/repeated phases or post-failure scoring."""
   177	    validate_lifecycle_receipt(receipt)
   178	    return _advance(receipt, stage, evidence)
   179	
   180	
   181	def require_scope(receipt: dict, *, run_id: str, manifest_sha256: str, scope_sha256: str) -> None:
   182	    """Check even incomplete checkpoints against their frozen publication scope."""
   183	    validate_lifecycle_receipt(receipt)
   184	    _require(receipt["binding"]["run_id"] == run_id
   185	             and receipt["binding"]["manifest_sha256"] == manifest_sha256
   186	             and receipt["scope_sha256"] == scope_sha256, "publication scope/binding changed")
   187	
   188	
   189	def require_publication(receipt: dict, *, run_id: str, manifest_sha256: str,
   190	                        scope_sha256: str, outcomes_sha256: str,
   191	                        cleanup_sha256: str, snapshot_sha256: str) -> None:
   192	    """Permit structural publication only for this exact scope and sealed result."""
   193	    require_scope(receipt, run_id=run_id, manifest_sha256=manifest_sha256, scope_sha256=scope_sha256)
   194	    _require(receipt["publication_ready"], "cleanup and snapshot lifecycle must pass before publication")
   195	    seal, cleanup = receipt["events"][-2:]
   196	    facts = seal["evidence"]["facts"]
   197	    _require(facts["outcomes_sha256"] == outcomes_sha256, "publication outcomes changed after seal")
   198	    _require(cleanup["receipt_sha256"] == cleanup_sha256
   199	             and digest(facts["snapshot"]) == snapshot_sha256,
   200	             "publication cleanup and snapshot attestations differ")

$ cat docs/enforcement-proof-decision.md
# Decision: enforcement-proof requirements are out of scope

**Decision (operator, 2026-10-15), following the `paired-filesystem-quotas` withdrawal of the
same day:** requirements whose *only* content is "prove that some enforcement mechanism is
really in force" are **not** preconditions for running a dataset. Three were reviewed under
that heading and withdrawn:

| Blocker | Where | What it demanded |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `official-scorer-identity` | shared | that source call sites *prove* fresh official evaluator execution and release/runtime identity |
| `snapshot-owned-cleanup` | shared | proof of bounded snapshots, exact owned absence and atomic lease closure |
| `prevent-platform-writes` | `scienceworld` | independent proof that mount enforcement is effective and platform provenance immutable |

## The reasoning, which is one argument applied three times

The platform already **performs** each of these; what was demanded was evidence that the
mechanism *cannot fail*. That is a different and much stronger claim, and it was raised in
each case without a measured failure behind it. The operator's position is that this is
over-defensive: the failure modes are not ones they have encountered in empirical work, and
the cost is that every dataset carries a gate that no amount of ordinary correct operation
can ever close.

The quota withdrawal is the worked example and the reason these three fell with it: the
requirement was not "make cleanup work", it was "prove cleanup cannot leak", and the price of
proving it was a root remount of two shared filesystems.

## What is still done, and must not be read as withdrawn

Withdrawing the *proof* requirement is not withdrawing the *behaviour*:

- scrapers and verifiers still rematerialize inputs, still snapshot and still clean up.
  `eval/admission/runner_admission.py::admit_snapshot` and `admit_cleanup` check available
  observations **best-effort, with uncertainty retained**. Missing or null facts, missing
  owned-object observations and incomplete namespace coverage do not by themselves refuse
  admission or quarantine a lease. Passing receipts explicitly record `best_effort=true`
  and `uncertainties`; they do not certify bounded peak snapshot usage, exact owned absence
  or atomic lease closure. A known failure or mismatch still refuses admission and is
  recorded in a blocked receipt; cleanup refusals still request `lease_action=quarantine`.
  Cleanup can request `lease_action=release` with recorded uncertainty. These functions
  inspect facts; they do not replace snapshot/cleanup execution or ledger persistence;
- each dataset review still records **metric identity** — what the number is and where it
  comes from — because that is what the reviews are for, and `official-scorer-identity`'s own
  `next_action` asked for documentation, not enforcement. The documentation continues; the
  blocker that gated the datasets on *proof* does not;
- the realtime refusal contract is untouched: a declared policy that needs a backend
  observation this checkout cannot supply still refuses before staging.

The snapshot/cleanup change preserves binding and input integrity: stale run/trial/manifest
bindings, malformed values, unexpected identities and invalid supplied hashes still refuse.
Omitted/null lifecycle facts, owned-object identity components and evidence hashes record
uncertainty; they are not invented successful observations. Known identity components must
still match the owned policy. An observed nonzero exit, OOM, running worker, deadline mismatch,
size/headroom violation, surviving owned object or nonempty owned namespace is still bad
even when other observations are missing. A passing admission receipt remains unmeasured
and grants no production authorization. Snapshot/cleanup uncertainty alone is not a new
dataset blocker, and it never repairs missing scores or failed historical runs.

Two adjacent checks in `eval/admission/pilot_admission.py` remain unchanged:
`scorer egress is not authorized` validates a pinned cache manifest (image identity/digest
and scorer network `none`) and catches mutation; `ownership label mismatch; refusing removal`
protects other leases' and users' resources. These are integrity and ownership properties,
not requirements to prove enforcement infallible. The independent pilot control and its
ledger retain their existing behavior; this change targets only the two runner admission APIs.

## What a reader should conclude

These mechanisms are **used, unproven, and accepted**. That is a materially different
statement from "verified", and it must not be upgraded in later summaries. If a mechanism is
ever observed to fail, the failure is the evidence that was missing, and the requirement can
be re-raised with something behind it.

The supporting reviews stay checked in and unedited, including
`docs/filesystem-quota-backends.md`, `reports/scorer-closure-codex170-report.md` and the
lifecycle material in `docs/task-233-quota-readback-authentication.md`.

$ sed -n 1,100p docs/enforcement-proof-decision.md
# Decision: enforcement-proof requirements are out of scope

**Decision (operator, 2026-10-15), following the `paired-filesystem-quotas` withdrawal of the
same day:** requirements whose *only* content is "prove that some enforcement mechanism is
really in force" are **not** preconditions for running a dataset. Three were reviewed under
that heading and withdrawn:

| Blocker | Where | What it demanded |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `official-scorer-identity` | shared | that source call sites *prove* fresh official evaluator execution and release/runtime identity |
| `snapshot-owned-cleanup` | shared | proof of bounded snapshots, exact owned absence and atomic lease closure |
| `prevent-platform-writes` | `scienceworld` | independent proof that mount enforcement is effective and platform provenance immutable |

## The reasoning, which is one argument applied three times

The platform already **performs** each of these; what was demanded was evidence that the
mechanism *cannot fail*. That is a different and much stronger claim, and it was raised in
each case without a measured failure behind it. The operator's position is that this is
over-defensive: the failure modes are not ones they have encountered in empirical work, and
the cost is that every dataset carries a gate that no amount of ordinary correct operation
can ever close.

The quota withdrawal is the worked example and the reason these three fell with it: the
requirement was not "make cleanup work", it was "prove cleanup cannot leak", and the price of
proving it was a root remount of two shared filesystems.

## What is still done, and must not be read as withdrawn

Withdrawing the *proof* requirement is not withdrawing the *behaviour*:

- scrapers and verifiers still rematerialize inputs, still snapshot and still clean up.
  `eval/admission/runner_admission.py::admit_snapshot` and `admit_cleanup` check available
  observations **best-effort, with uncertainty retained**. Missing or null facts, missing
  owned-object observations and incomplete namespace coverage do not by themselves refuse
  admission or quarantine a lease. Passing receipts explicitly record `best_effort=true`
  and `uncertainties`; they do not certify bounded peak snapshot usage, exact owned absence
  or atomic lease closure. A known failure or mismatch still refuses admission and is
  recorded in a blocked receipt; cleanup refusals still request `lease_action=quarantine`.
  Cleanup can request `lease_action=release` with recorded uncertainty. These functions
  inspect facts; they do not replace snapshot/cleanup execution or ledger persistence;
- each dataset review still records **metric identity** — what the number is and where it
  comes from — because that is what the reviews are for, and `official-scorer-identity`'s own
  `next_action` asked for documentation, not enforcement. The documentation continues; the
  blocker that gated the datasets on *proof* does not;
- the realtime refusal contract is untouched: a declared policy that needs a backend
  observation this checkout cannot supply still refuses before staging.

The snapshot/cleanup change preserves binding and input integrity: stale run/trial/manifest
bindings, malformed values, unexpected identities and invalid supplied hashes still refuse.
Omitted/null lifecycle facts, owned-object identity components and evidence hashes record
uncertainty; they are not invented successful observations. Known identity components must
still match the owned policy. An observed nonzero exit, OOM, running worker, deadline mismatch,
size/headroom violation, surviving owned object or nonempty owned namespace is still bad
even when other observations are missing. A passing admission receipt remains unmeasured
and grants no production authorization. Snapshot/cleanup uncertainty alone is not a new
dataset blocker, and it never repairs missing scores or failed historical runs.

Two adjacent checks in `eval/admission/pilot_admission.py` remain unchanged:
`scorer egress is not authorized` validates a pinned cache manifest (image identity/digest
and scorer network `none`) and catches mutation; `ownership label mismatch; refusing removal`
protects other leases' and users' resources. These are integrity and ownership properties,
not requirements to prove enforcement infallible. The independent pilot control and its
ledger retain their existing behavior; this change targets only the two runner admission APIs.

## What a reader should conclude

These mechanisms are **used, unproven, and accepted**. That is a materially different
statement from "verified", and it must not be upgraded in later summaries. If a mechanism is
ever observed to fail, the failure is the evidence that was missing, and the requirement can
be re-raised with something behind it.

The supporting reviews stay checked in and unedited, including
`docs/filesystem-quota-backends.md`, `reports/scorer-closure-codex170-report.md` and the
lifecycle material in `docs/task-233-quota-readback-authentication.md`.
